The acquisition of Reebok in 2006 reinforced the company’s position in the sports market, and partnerships with designers such as Yohji Yamamoto and Stella McCartney added a high-fashion dimension to the brand.\n\n- Key milestones in the adidas brand’s evolution\n\n1. 1949: Adi Dassler establishes adidas and introduces the three-stripe logo.\n2. 1954: The German national football team wins the World Cup wearing adidas’ screw-in stud shoes, further bolstering the brand’s reputation in sports innovation.\n3. 1970: Adidas becomes the official ball supplier for the FIFA World Cup.\n4. 1980s-1990s: The brand expands from sports shoes into the broader sportswear and equipment market, including apparel, accessories, and sports equipment.\n5. 2006: Acquisition of Reebok bolsters the adidas Group’s presence within North America and strengthens its position in the global sports market.\n6. 2010s: Continued expansion of adidas’ product offerings, with notable partnerships including Y-3 with Yohji Yamamoto, collaboration with Kanye West on Yeezy, and partnerships with high-profile athletes such as Lionel Messi and James Harden.\n7. 2015: Introduction of the Parley for the Oceans initiative, showcasing adidas’ commitment to sustainability by using ocean plastic waste to create performance footwear and apparel.\n8. 2020: Adidas announces a partnership with Allbirds, another sustainability-focused footwear brand, aimed at creating the lowest carbon footprint sports shoe possible.\n\n- The adidas brand’s mission, values, and beliefs\n\nAdidas’ mission is to be the best sports company in the world, expressing this through the brand’s three-pillar strategy: Speed, Key Cities, and Open Source. These pillars aim to foster innovation, focus on crucial urban markets, and encourage collaboration among athletes, consumers, and partners.\n\nThe brand seeks to empower people through sports by adhering to its core values – passion, integrity, innovation, and teamwork. These values are reflected in numerous initiatives, including sustainable product design, inclusive sizing and representation in campaigns, and investments in employee development and well-being.\n\nAdidas is a brand that believes in the transformative power of sports, working tirelessly to create products and experiences that enable people to harness this power and improve their lives.\n\n- Visual representation using elements of the brand heritage model\n\nAn effective framework for examining adidas’ brand heritage is Aaker’s Brand System Model. It comprises four components: Brand Identity, Brand Meaning, Brand Response, and Brand Relationship. Brand Identity (Adidas Logo and Three-Stripe Mark): Adidas’ iconic logo, featuring three stripes, represents the unity, quality, and performance associated with the brand.\n2. Brand Meaning (Innovation, Quality, and Passion for Sports): The key elements of adidas’ brand meaning include innovation in sports equipment and apparel, high-quality products, and a genuine love for sports in all forms.\n3. landscapes. Jaguar Land Rover, with its focus on luxury vehicles, may need to consider cultural nuances in design and marketing to appeal to different ethnic groups.\n\n**Social Structures:** Social hierarchies and power dynamics vary across cultures. Understanding these structures is crucial for effective business interactions. In some cultures, decision-making is centralized, while others emphasize consensus-building. Marks & Spencer, known for its hierarchical structure, may need to adapt its approach in cultures that value more egalitarian decision-making processes. Jaguar Land Rover, with its global supply chain, must be sensitive to varying social structures when negotiating with suppliers and partners.\n\n**Religion:** Religious beliefs and practices can significantly impact consumer behavior and business practices. Marks & Spencer avoids selling alcohol in some countries due to religious sensitivities. Jaguar Land Rover, while not directly affected by religious restrictions, may need to be mindful of religious holidays and observances when scheduling meetings or events.\n\n**Values:** Cultural values shape attitudes towards work, time, and success. In some cultures, punctuality is highly valued, while in others, a more relaxed approach to time is the norm. Marks & Spencer, with its emphasis on efficiency and customer service, may need to adapt its expectations in cultures where a more relaxed pace is the norm. Jaguar Land Rover, known for its precision engineering, may need to communicate its values clearly to ensure alignment with local partners and employees.\n\n**Attitudes to Business:** Cultural perceptions of business ethics, competition, and risk-taking can vary significantly. Marks & Spencer, with its focus on employee well-being, may need to adapt its policies to align with local norms. Jaguar Land Rover, with its global operations, may need to adopt a more flexible management style to accommodate diverse cultural preferences.\n\n**Workforce Practices:** Cultural norms influence work practices, such as working hours, vacation time, and communication styles. These differences can significantly impact various aspects of their operations, from communication to consumer behavior.\\n\\n**Language:** Language is the most obvious cultural barrier. While English is widely spoken in business, nuances in language, idioms, and even humor can lead to misunderstandings. Marks & Spencer, for example, has faced challenges translating its marketing materials accurately, sometimes resulting in unintended comedic effects. Jaguar Land Rover, with its global manufacturing and sales network, invests heavily in multilingual communication and training to ensure clear and effective communication across its diverse workforce and customer base.\\n\\n**Ethnic Make-up of Populations:** The ethnic composition of a country influences consumer preferences, cultural sensitivities, and even legal frameworks. Marks & Spencer has adapted its product offerings to cater to diverse tastes, introducing halal food options in Muslim-majority countries and adjusting clothing styles to suit local preferences. Jaguar Land Rover, with its focus on luxury vehicles, may need to consider cultural nuances in design and marketing to appeal to different ethnic groups.\\n\\n**Social Structures:** Social hierarchies and power dynamics vary across cultures. Marks & Spencer, known for its hierarchical structure, may need to adapt its approach in cultures that value more egalitarian decision-making processes. Jaguar Land Rover, with its global supply chain, must be sensitive to varying social structures when negotiating with suppliers and partners.\\n\\n**Religion:** Religious beliefs and practices can significantly impact consumer behavior and business practices. Marks & Spencer avoids selling alcohol in some countries due to religious sensitivities. Jaguar Land Rover, while not directly affected by religious restrictions, may need to be mindful of religious holidays and observances when scheduling meetings or events.\\n\\n**Values:** Cultural values shape attitudes towards work, time, and success. Marks & Spencer, with its emphasis on efficiency and customer service, may need to adapt its expectations in cultures where a more relaxed pace is the norm. Jaguar Land Rover, known for its precision engineering, may need to communicate its values clearly to ensure alignment with local partners and employees.\\n\\n**Attitudes to Business:** Cultural perceptions of business ethics, competition, and risk-taking can vary significantly. Marks & Spencer, with its strong ethical reputation, may need to navigate different expectations regarding corporate social responsibility in different markets. Jaguar Land Rover, operating in a competitive industry, must be aware of local business practices and ensure its operations adhere to ethical standards.\\n\\n**Attitudes to Work:** Work-life balance, employee motivation, and expectations regarding authority vary across cultures. Jaguar Land Rover, with its complex global supply chain, must be aware of cultural differences in contract interpretation and enforcement.\\n\\n**Composition of the Workforce:** Cultural diversity can enrich a company's workforce, bringing a variety of perspectives and skills. Jaguar Land Rover, with its engineering-focused workforce, may need to consider cultural differences in recruitment and training to attract and retain talent from diverse backgrounds.\\n\\n**Management Style:** Effective management styles vary across cultures. Some cultures value hierarchical structures and top-down decision-making, while others prefer more collaborative and participative approaches. Marks & Spencer, with its traditional hierarchical structure, may need to adapt its management style in cultures that value more egalitarian leadership. Jaguar Land Rover, with its global operations, may need to adopt a more flexible management style to accommodate diverse cultural preferences.\\n\\n**Workforce Practices:** Cultural norms influence work practices, such as working hours, vacation time, and communication styles. 不行，L占了)。重新构建：若N在周四，S不能在周三、周五。S可选周一、周二。若S在周一，剩下L, O, H, P。L必须在周三。O必须在H前一天。H不能在周五。若H在周二，O在周一（冲突，S在周一）。若H在周三（冲突，L在周三）。若H在周四（冲突，N在周四）。若H在周五（禁止）。所以S不能在周一？让我们仔细推演。N=Thu。S != Wed, Fri。S = Mon or Tue。Case 1: S=Mon。剩余位置Tue, Wed, Thu(N), Fri。剩余社区L, O, H, P。L必须在Wed。所以Wed=L。剩余Tue, Fri给O, H, P。O必须在H前一天。若H=Tue，O=Mon(冲突，S=Mon)。若H=Fri，禁止。若H=Wed(冲突，L=Wed)。若H=Thu(冲突，N=Thu)。所以S不能是Mon？Case 2: S=Tue。剩余位置Mon, Wed, Thu(N), Fri。剩余社区L, O, H, P。L必须在Wed。所以Wed=L。剩余Mon, Fri给O, H, P。O必须在H前一天。若H=Mon，O=Sun(不可能)。若H=Fri，禁止。若H=Wed(冲突)。若H=Thu(冲突)。这似乎意味着N在周四时，没有任何合法排期？让我重新检查规则。规则：1. HH in Mon-Fri, not Fri. 2. If OV visited, OV day before HH. 3. If LV visited, LV on Wed. 4. NT and SS both visited, not consecutive. 5. One neighborhood per day. 5 days. 6 neighborhoods. 1 left out. 如果N=Thu。S != Wed, Fri。S可以是Mon, Tue。如果S=Mon。剩余：Tue, Wed, Thu(N), Fri。需放L, O, H, P。L=Wed。剩Tue, Fri。需放O, H, P中的两个？不对，总共5天。已定：Mon(S), Wed(L), Thu(N)。剩Tue, Fri。需放O, H, P中的两个？不对，一共要选5个社区。已选S, L, N。还需选2个。从O, H, P中选2个。O必须在H前一天。可能的组合：(O, H) = (Tue, Wed)? 但Wed是L。不行。(O, H) = (Mon, Tue)? Mon是S。不行。(O, H) = (Fri, Sat)? 不行。所以如果S=Mon，无法放置O和H。因此S不能是Mon。如果S=Tue。已定：Tue(S), Wed(L), Thu(N)。剩Mon, Fri。需放O, H, P中的两个。O必须在H前一天。可能的(O, H) = (Mon, Tue)? Tue是S。不行。(O, H) = (Fri, Sat)? 不行。所以S也不能是Tue。这意味着如果N在周四，根本找不到合法排期？这说明前提'If Nottingham is visited on Thursday'会导致矛盾，即这是一个不可能的情况。但是题目问的是'which one of the following must be true?'，通常这类LSAT题目隐含前提是可以实现的。难道我漏了什么？啊，P可以替换O或H吗？如果不选O呢？规则说'If Oldtown is visited...'，没说必须访问O。规则只说'Hidden Hills is visited'。'Nottingham and Sunnyside are both visited'。所以必须访问N, S, H, L。必须访问的有：H, N, S, L。共4个。第5个可以是O或P。如果必须访问H，且H不在周五。如果N在周四。S != Wed, Fri。S = Mon or Tue。必须访问H。H不能在周五。H不能在Thu(N)。H不能在Wed(L)。H只能在Mon或Tue。如果H=Mon，O必须在Sun(不可能)。所以H不能是Mon。如果H=Tue，O必须在Mon。此时：Mon=O, Tue=H, Wed=L, Thu=N。S不能是Wed, Fri。S可以是Mon? 冲突(O)。S可以是Tue? 冲突(H)。S可以是Thu? 冲突(N)。S可以是Fri? 禁止相邻。所以S无处可去。如果H=Wed? 冲突L。如果H=Thu? 冲突N。如果H=Fri? 禁止。所以，如果N在周四，确实无法构造合法排期。这意味着前提'If Nottingham is visited on Thursday'是一个假命题，或者说在逻辑题中，如果前提导致无解，那么任何结论都可以被认为是真的（空真），或者题目出错了。但在LSAT中，通常不会出现这种情况。让我重新检查规则。'Nottingham and Sunnyside are both visited, but not on consecutive days.' 'If Lakeville is visited, then it is visited on Wednesday.' 'Hidden Hills is visited'. 'If Oldtown is visited, then it is visited on the day immediately before Hidden Hills is visited.' 必须访问的集合：{H, N, S, L}。第5个是O或P。如果N=Thu。S != Wed, Fri。S = Mon, Tue。H != Fri。H != Thu。H != Wed (if L=Wed)。如果L=Wed。H只能是Mon, Tue。若H=Mon -> O=Sun (No)。若H=Tue -> O=Mon。此时Mon=O, Tue=H, Wed=L, Thu=N。S必须存在。S不能是Mon(O), Tue(H), Wed(L), Thu(N), Fri(Consecutive to N)。所以S无处可放。如果L不访问？规则说'If Lakeville is visited...'，没说必须访问L。啊！规则只说了'Hidden Hills is visited', 'Nottingham and Sunnyside are both visited'。没说必须访问Lakeville。也没说必须访问Oldtown。必须访问的是：H, N, S。共3个。剩下2个从L, O, P中选。如果N=Thu。S != Wed, Fri。S = Mon, Tue。H != Fri。H != Thu。H可以是Mon, Tue, Wed。Case A: L不访问。必须选O和P。H, N, S, O, P。N=Thu。S=Mon或Tue。H必须在O之后一天。若O=Mon, H=Tue。此时：Mon=O, Tue=H, Thu=N。S不能是Mon(O), Tue(H), Wed(空闲), Thu(N), Fri(Consecutive)。S可以是Wed。检查：S=Wed。N=Thu。相邻？Yes, Wed and Thu are consecutive。所以S不能是Wed。S不能是Fri(Consecutive)。S不能是Mon, Tue, Thu。所以S无处可放。若O=Tue, H=Wed。此时：Tue=O, Wed=H, Thu=N。S不能是Tue, Wed, Thu, Fri。S可以是Mon。检查：S=Mon, N=Thu。不相邻。可行。排期：Mon=S, Tue=O, Wed=H, Thu=N, Fri=P。检查约束：H visited, not Fri (Wed). Differences and International Businesses:\n\nNavigating the global marketplace requires international businesses like Marks & Spencer and Jaguar Land Rover to understand and adapt to diverse cultural landscapes. These differences can significantly impact various aspects of their operations, from communication to consumer behavior.\n\n**Language:** Language is the most obvious cultural barrier. While English is widely spoken in business, nuances in language, idioms, and even humor can lead to misunderstandings. Marks & Spencer, for example, has faced challenges translating its marketing materials accurately, sometimes resulting in unintended comedic effects. Jaguar Land Rover, with its global manufacturing and sales network, invests heavily in multilingual communication and training to ensure clear and effective communication across its diverse workforce and customer base.\n\n**Ethnic Make-up of Populations:** The ethnic composition of a country influences consumer preferences, cultural sensitivities, and even legal frameworks. Marks & Spencer has adapted its product offerings to cater to diverse tastes, introducing halal food options in Muslim-majority countries and adjusting clothing styles to suit local preferences. Jaguar Land Rover, with its focus on luxury vehicles, may need to consider cultural nuances in design and marketing to appeal to different ethnic groups.\n\n**Social Structures:** Social hierarchies and power dynamics vary across cultures. Understanding these structures is crucial for effective business interactions. In some cultures, decision-making is centralized, while others emphasize consensus-building. Marks & Spencer, known for its hierarchical structure, may need to adapt its approach in cultures that value more egalitarian decision-making processes. Jaguar Land Rover, with its global supply chain, must be sensitive to varying social structures when negotiating with suppliers and partners.\n\n**Religion:** Religious beliefs and practices can significantly impact consumer behavior and business practices. Marks & Spencer avoids selling alcohol in some countries due to religious sensitivities. Jaguar Land Rover, while not directly affected by religious restrictions, may need to be mindful of religious holidays and observances when scheduling meetings or events.\n\n**Values:** Cultural values shape attitudes towards work, time, and success. In some cultures, punctuality is highly valued, while in others, a more relaxed approach to time is the norm. Marks & Spencer, with its emphasis on efficiency and customer service, may need to adapt its expectations in cultures where a more relaxed pace is the norm. Jaguar Land Rover, known for its precision engineering, may need to communicate its values clearly to ensure alignment with local partners and employees.\n\n**Attitudes to Business:** Cultural perceptions of business ethics, competition, and risk-taking can vary significantly. Marks & Spencer, with its strong ethical reputation, may need to navigate different expectations regarding corporate social responsibility in different markets. Jaguar Land Rover, operating in a competitive industry, must be aware of local business practices and ensure its operations adhere to ethical standards.\n\n**Attitudes to Work:** Work-life balance, employee motivation, and expectations regarding authority vary across cultures. Marks & Spencer, with its focus on employee well-being, may need to adapt its policies to align with local norms. Jaguar Land Rover, with its global workforce, must be sensitive to cultural differences in work ethic and expectations.\n\n**Openness to Change:** Some cultures are more receptive to innovation and change than others. Marks & Spencer, known for its adaptability, may need to tailor its approach to introducing new products or services in markets resistant to change. Jaguar Land Rover, constantly innovating in the automotive industry, must be mindful of cultural attitudes towards technological advancements.\n\n\n\n## Impact of Cultural Differences on International Businesses:\n\nCultural differences can significantly impact various aspects of international business operations. Understanding these nuances is crucial for success in the global marketplace.\n\n**Contract Negotiations:** Cultural differences can influence negotiation styles, communication patterns, and expectations regarding contracts. For example, in some cultures, direct and assertive negotiation tactics are preferred, while in others, a more indirect and relationship-based approach is more effective. Marks & Spencer, known for its formal and structured approach to negotiations, may need to adapt its style in cultures where building personal relationships is paramount. Jaguar Land Rover, with its complex global supply chain, must be aware of cultural differences in contract interpretation and enforcement.\n\n**Composition of the Workforce:** Cultural diversity can enrich a company's workforce, bringing a variety of perspectives and skills. However, it also presents challenges in terms of communication, team dynamics, and management styles. Marks & Spencer, with its global presence, actively promotes diversity and inclusion initiatives to create a welcoming and inclusive work environment. Jaguar Land Rover, with its engineering-focused workforce, may need to consider cultural differences in recruitment and training to attract and retain talent from diverse backgrounds.\n\n**Management Style:** Effective management styles vary across cultures. Some cultures value hierarchical structures and top-down decision-making, while others prefer more collaborative and participative approaches.
